2430. HENRY DAWSON was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of October, 23 yards of merino, value 1l. 4s., the goods of Uriah Macey.
JOHN ROBERT HOLMES. I am apprentice to Mr. Uriah Macey, linen-draper, Great Titchbourne-street. On the 3rd of October, about half-past eight o'clock, I heard a noise in the shop, that drew my attention to the door; I went out, and saw the prisoner with a piece of merino under his arm- I followed him up Foley-place; when I got within a few yards of him, he threw it down; I picked it up, and gave an alarm - Powell followed and took him; I am sure he is the person - it was taken from within the shop, near the door.
WILLIAM POWELL. I was standing in my shop in Foley-place, and heard a cry of Stop thief! I looked out and saw the prisoner running away, and Holmes pursuing him; I saw him throw down the merino - I pursued, and brought him back.
WILLIAM MARSHALL (Police-constable E 31). I took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to)
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18321018-224]
2431. WATTS EDWARDS and GEORGE ROGERS were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of October, 1 basket, value 1s., and 40 eggs, value 2s. 6d., the goods of John Brown.
MICHAEL SCANTLING (Police-constable G 118). I saw the prisoner in Radnor-street, City-road, on the 15th of October; I saw Edwards running, and carrying this basket, which was covered with this apron - Rogers was behind him, but had nothing; it was between five and six hundred yards from the prosecutor's - I took Edwards, and Lapthorn took Rogers; I found the owner of the property.
JOHN LAPTHORN (Police-constable C 102). I saw the prisoners running together; I heard Edwards say to Rogers "Come along" - we run after them; Rogers run off, I followed and took him.
MARGARET BROWN. I am the wife of John Brown. I lost a basket with eggs; as near as I can tell this is it-I missed it from my window, which was open, when the officer asked me; I saw a boy in the evening looking through the window - I cannot tell if it was one of the prisoners.
Edwards put in a written defence stating that they had met a man who had employed them to carry the basket; he received a good character.
EDWARDS - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined One Month.
ROGERS - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321018-225]
2432. HENRIETTA MARIA FRANKLIN was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of October, 1 handkerchief, value 5s., the goods of Joseph Usher Still, from his person.
JOSEPH USHER STILL. I attend on insane persons, and live in Derby-row, Kingsland. On the 12th of October, between two and three o'clock in the morning, I was going home - I had been out with some friends, and drank a little, but nothing of any consequence; I fell in with the prisoner in Kingsland-road - I walked up Old-street-road with her- I sat down and took her on my knee; when she had been with me a quarter of an hour, she left me suddenly-I missed my handkerchief in about twenty minutes from my right hand coat pocket; I had not given her any money, nor did she ask me for any - I had seen her once before, but was not acquainted with her; I did not give her the handkerchief.
RICHARD HUNT (Police-constable H 70). I took the prisoner and found the handkerchief partly in her pocket; she at first denied having it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. He promised to give me something and did not.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321018-226]
OLD COURT. WEDNESDAY, OCTOBER 24.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
2433. WILLIAM WOOD was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of October, 9 shillings, and 1 penny, the monies of John Tucker.
CHARLOTTE TUCKER. I am the wife of Joseph Tucker; we keep a coal-shed in Cumberland-street, Middlesexhospital. The prisoner came there in the afternoon of the 12th of October, between three and four o'clock, and asked me to send 1/2 cwt. of coals, and 1d. worth of wood,with change for half a sovereign, to No. 11, Tottenham-street - he went away, returned, complained that I had not sent them, and said he would wait and go with the boy; I gave the boy the coals with 9s. 1d. - I told him to give him the change, and bring me back the halfsovereign.
JOSEPH TUCKER. My mother sent me with the coals, and 9s. 1d. - I went with the prisoner nearly to Tottenham-court-road; he then asked for the change, and said he would give me the half-sovereign - I gave him the change, which I would not have done, but he said he would give me the half-sovereign, and he ran away with it - I put the coals down, ran after him, calling Stop thief! and he was taken.
Prisoner. I did not walk fast - he put it into my hand. Witness. He asked me for it.
RALPH JACOBS. I live in Church-street, Shoreditch. I was in Tottenham-court-road - the prisoner run by me; Tucker called Stop thief! - I saw the prisoner stopped, and pushed down; he got up and ran again, but was secured and searched, but the money not found; I at last said I would strip him but what I would find it - he said"Well, if you will forgive me, I will give you the money;" he had then got 9s. 1d. in his hand.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321018-227]
2434. WILLIAM WALLACE was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of September, 1 coat, value 5s., the goods of Charles Thomas Charlwood.
CHARLES THOMAS CHARLWOOD. On the 29th of September I left my great coat in a cart, in Tavistock-place, Covent-garden; I lost it, and afterwards saw it in Norton's hands - this is it.
JOSEPH NORTON (Police-constable F 44). On the 29th of September, about half-past five o'clock in the morning, I stopped the prisoner in Little Russell-street, with this coat under his arm - he said it was his own.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in Covent-garden, and saw the coat on the ground, at the horse's heels.
CHARLES THOMAS CHARLWOOD. It could not have fallen at the horse's heels; it was in the body of a large cart.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-228]
2435. MARY WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing on the 3rd of October, 1 pair of boots, value 30s.; 1 waistcoat, value 10s., and 2 handkerchiefs, value 3s., the goods of Benjamin Wurr.
BENJAMIN WURR. I live at Chelsea, and work at a stable. I met the prisoner on the 2nd of October, I went with her to George-street, St. Giles', and went to bed with her - when I awoke in the morning she was gone; I missed my boots, waistcoat, and two handkerchiefs.
Prisoner. He told me to pawn them for what he had promised me, as he had but 21/2d. Witness I did not; she took them while I was asleep - I do not know whether she lodged there.
WILLIAM WOODMAN (Police-constable F 9). On the 23rd of October, between five and six o'clock in the morning, I stopped the prisoner in Drury-lane with a bundle, containing a pair of boots, a waistcoat, and handkerchief - the other handkerchief was tied round her thigh; she said they were her own, and that she had pawned a ring to get them out of pawn.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He gave me them to pawn, and said William should fetch them back; I have known him two years.
BENJAMIN WURR. I knew her two years ago, when she lived at the White Hart, Holborn - she never pawned any thing for me; I never lived with her - I was sober.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-229]
Before Mr. Recorder.
2436. JAMES YOUNG was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of October, 1 painting, value 2l., and 1 pictureframe, value 15s., the goods of Alfred Weaver.
JAMES HORNING. I am shopman to Alfred Weaver, carver and gilder, Brompton-row, Knightsbridge. On the 16th of October, between eight and nine o'clock in the morning, I saw the prisoner going out of the shop with this painting in his hand - the moment he saw me he attempted to put it on the table, but it fell down; he ran out - I pursued him about fifty yards, calling Stop thief! and he was stopped - the shop door was open,
JACOB McCARTHY. I am a Policeman. I saw the prisoner running, and assisted in securing him; as soon as he heard a cry of Stop thief! and the people running after him, so that he could not get away, he stopped.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had merely taken the painting up to look at.
JAMES HORNING. I was in the workshop, under the shop where the painting hung - I heard somebody over head, ran up, and saw him going out with it; he was on the step of the door, but on seeing me he put it down.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18321018-230]
2437. WILLIAM CHAPMAN and JAMES HARRIS were indicted for stealing, on the 18th of October, 1 watch, value 25s., and 1 key, value 1s., the goods of Barbara Folkard.
BARBARA FOLKARD. I am a widow, and keep a shop in William-street, Manchester-square. On the 18th of October, about eight o'clock in the morning, I saw Harris in my shop - he asked to look at an old tea-saucer; he then looked at something else - I was engaged with him about ten minutes, and he left without buying any thing - my watch hung on the parlour mantel-piece; I do not know whether the private door was open, but if it was, any body could get into the parlour - a neighbour came over and gave me information; I went into the parlour, and the watch was gone - Harris had left then, and in about an hour I saw the prisoners in custody with it - they were strangers.
Harris. Q. Are you sure it was I who came to your shop? A. He is the man.
THOMAS COCHRAN. I am a saddler, and live nearly opposite Folkard; about ten minutes to eight o'clock in the morning, I saw the prisoners lurking about the street, in company together - I watched them; one of them looked through the key-hole of the private door; they then went on down the street together, and returned; the prosecutrix was sweeping the front of hershop; as they passed, one pushed the other, as if to say, go in at the private door, which was then open; they went on, then came back, and the door was put too - I do not know whether it was shut; Chapman looked through the key-hole, then opened the door, and went in; Harris went in at the shop door; I ran down the street to look for a Policeman, but could not find one, and on returning, I told my employer what I had seen; Chapman came out at the private door, and Harris at the shop door; my employer went over, and told the prosecutrix; the prosecutrix's son and I went after them - we could not find them; we met a Policeman, and described them - he afterwards took them; I am certain of them both - the watch was found on Chapman.
Harris. Q. Did you see me with Chapman after I came out? A. Chapman stopped and spoke to two others at the corner, and Harris went by, and did not speak to him; but they were in company together when we took them.
WILLIAM HOOKER. I am a Policeman. I received information, and went to Lisson-grove, about a mile from the house, and saw the prisoners going along; Cochran pointed them out - I ran; Harris turned round, saw me, and stopped as if for a necessary purpose; I told Cochran to take him; I followed, and took Chapman - I saw the watch-key in his mouth, and caught hold of it - we struggled; I forced his mouth open, and believe he must have swallowed it; I saw another watch-key hanging from his pocket - I caught hold of it; he said, "It is mine, what do you want with it?" I found the watch in that pocket, and in Harris's pocket I found a skeleton and a latch-key; Harris said, "I know nothing of it, I have not been with him."(Property produced and sworn to).
Chapman's Defence. About five minutes before I was taken, I met Harris, and asked him to go across the fields with me - he did not know what I had in my pocket.
Harris' Defence. I unfortunately met Chapman about five minutes before; I had not seen him all the morning before; he asked me to cross the fields with him on an errand; I turned and saw two Policemen, but did not think they were after me, and stopped.
CHAPMAN - GUILTY. Aged 20.
HARRIS - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-231]
2438. EDWARD FOLEY and WILLIAM BRIDGE were indicted for stealing, on the 5th of October, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Charles Warton, from his person.
CHARLES WARTON. I am an auctioneer, and live in Threadneedle-street. On the 5th of October, about ten o'clock in the morning, I was in New-street, Covent-garden, and had my handkerchief in my pocket; Ruffy gave me information, and I missed it - I saw Bridge there, but not Foley.
JOHN RUFFY. I am an oilman, and live in James-street, Covent-garden. I was in New-street, and saw the prisoners together; my attention was attracted by Foley beckoning to a boy behind him - I watched, and saw Bridge take a handkerchief from the prosecutor's pocket, and put it behind him; Foley was alongside him, and could not help seeing it - I informed the prosecutor, and then pursued; they took different directions - Bridge dropped the handkerchief in the middle of the road, and was taken directly after by the Policeman, who took it up- Foley was brought to the station in about half an hour; I had not seen him do any thing.
THOMAS POCOCK. I am a Policeman. I heard a cry of Stop thief! and saw Bridge running; he put his hand into his bosom, and threw the handkerchief away; I took it up and secured him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Foley's Defence. I was going to the workhouse for my mother's money, and had the ticket in my hand.
THOMAS POCOCK. He had the ticket in his pocket, and said as he does now.
Bridge's Defence. I met this boy, and asked where he was going; I was by the gentleman, but never touched him.
BRIDGE - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
FOLEY - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321018-232]
2439. HENRY BARR and THOMAS SMITH were indicted for stealing, on the 18th of October, 2 pistols, value 18s., the goods of Abraham Edmund Reynolds.
RICHARD PEACHY. I am servant to Abraham Edmund Reynolds, of Mile-end-road - these pistols were on a stall-board, in the street; they were taken between eleven and twelve o'clock - I found them at the station.
JOHN GAMBLE. I am a bottle-merchant, and live in Globe-road. On the 18th of October I was in my cart, in Mile-end-road, and saw Smith take these pistols off Reynolds' board, which was about three yards from the house- he gave them to Barr; I jumped out of my cart, and informed the prosecutor - we pursued, and Smith was taken in the fields; Barr was taken by a Policeman - they were both taken within ten minutes.
THOMAS MARSHALL. I am a Policeman. I apprehended Barr, with the two pistols wrapped in his apron.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Barr's Defence. I happened to meet this young gentleman - he snatched the pistols off the board, and gave them to me.
BARR - GUILTY. Aged 19.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18321018-233]
2440. JULIA DWYER and ESTHER PAGE were indicted for stealing, on the 4th of October, 2 sheets, value 12s., the goods of William Matthias Spooner; and 1 gown, value 5s., the goods of Eliza Smith .
WILLIAM MATTHIAS SPOONER. I live in Bath-place, New-road. On Wednesday night, the 3rd of October, when I went to bed, my house was all safe - Smith, my servant, alarmed me at seven o'clock in the morning, and property amounting to 4l. or 5l. was stolen; the sheets had been washed and laid in a basket in the kitchen, with the other linen - I knew nothing of the prisoners; I went down on hearing the alarm, and found the premises had been forcibly entered, the kitchen in confusion, the linen tumbled about, and the drawers and every placerifled - I went and gave information at the station-house, and was informed the prisoners had been stopped in another district; and at eleven o'clock I saw my property at the office.
ELIZA SMITH. I am in the prosecutor's employ. I got up at seven o'clock in the morning, and on opening the parlour shutters saw a shirt and tablecloth in the garden - they were in the kitchen the night before, in a basket of linen ready for the mangle; I missed two sheets and my gown, which I found at the office that morning - two doors had been forced open, the frame and all forced in.(Property produced and sworn to.)
DAVID LAING. I am a Policeman. I stopped the prisoners at a quarter to one o'clock in the morning, walking arm-in-arm, in Fitzroy-street, about one hundred yards from the prosecutor's - I asked twice what they had got; Dwyer said, "I have got some things;" I said, "What things?" she said, "Some things which belong to the other girl's mother;" I opened her apron, and found two damp sheets, and on Page I found two gowns: I found no housebreaking implements on them.
Dwyer's Defence. I am an unfortunate girl, and knew this girl by buying oysters of her; we walked together-I saw this parcel, and took it up.
Page's Defence. I met her - she said she was could and hungry; I got her some victuals, and, as we walked along, picked the bundle up.
Page received a good character.
DWYER - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
PAGE - GUILTY. Aged 25.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321018-234]
2441. JOSEPH HOSKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of October, 13 forks, value 7s., and 12 knives, value 7s., the goods of John Abrahams ; and JAMES LOCK was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing them to have been stolen.
ALICE ABRAHAMS. I am the wife of Isaac Abrahams, a jeweller and diamond merchant; he is a lunatic - I live with my father, in Vinegar-yard, Drury-lane. Hoskins was my father's servant, and lived in the house - this property belonged to my husband; I saw the knives and forks safe about ten days before he left, which he did very late on the night of the 10th of October, without notice, and in a day or two missed these things - I saw them at the office, when the prisoners were taken - when Hoskins was taken he said he was not sorry he was taken, and could tell me where the property was; in consequence of what he said Lock was taken.
BENJAMIN KEY. I am an oil and colourman. Last Saturday week, at night, Lock came to my shop, and offered me eleven knives and twelve forks for sale, with a carving knife and fork - he asked 10s. for them, which I gave him; he was in the habit of coming backwards and forwards - I have known him thirteen years, and believed him particularly honest; he kept a marine-store shop in White Hart-yard.
GEORGE MAY. I am a Policeman. I apprehended Lock; he said he had bought some knives and forks of Hoskins, and gave him 2s. 6d. and the duplicate of a silk handkerchief for them. I took him to the prosecutor's and he said the same there.
Lock. They were not in the state they are now - I had a deal of trouble to clean them; they were in a shocking state.
ALICE ABRAHAMS. I have had them a long time, but they were not much used; they were perfectly clean when taken - when Lock was brought to our house, he said, rather than have any bother he would pay for them - I value them at 14s.
Lock's Defence. He came to my shop, and asked 4s. for them, which I gave him; I might be out of my judgment, but I never assoicated with thieves.
HOSKINS - GUILTY - Aged 19.
LOCK - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321018-273]
2442. JOSEPH HOSKINS was again indicted for stealing, on the 9th of October, 1 watch, value 2l.; 1 watchguard, value 10s.; 1 pair of shoes, value 10s.; and 6 handkerchiefs, value 6s.; the goods of Emanuel Moses, his master; and JOHN SMITH, alias BAKER , was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing them to have been stolen.
EMANUEL MOSES. I keep the Black Lion, Vinegaryard, Drury-lane . Hoskins was in my employ; he left on Wednesday, the 10th of October, a little before twelve o'clock at night; he had half a crown a week; and had been paid on the Monday; I sent my wife up stairs when we missed him, and the watch and other things were missing - I had seen the watch an hour before - I saw the shoes last Saturday at Queen-square, where Smith was in custody - my shoes are patent ones, such as cannot be found in London.
ALICE ABRAHAMS. I heard the prisoner going up stairs about eleven o'clock at night - I was in bed on the second-floor, and said, "Joe, is that you?" he said Yes - I heard him open the door of the room this property was in; and as he came down, seeing a light through the crack of the door, I said, "How is it you have a light?" as we never allow one - he said he had been to take some things up stairs, and in about an hour I heard of the robbery - when Hoskins was taken, he said he gave the property to Baker to sell, in Field-lane - I had seen the watch that evening, and had it in my hand - six handkerchiefs were missing from a lumber-room, opposite the room the watch was in.
ROSINA JONES. On the Wednesday night Hoskins left his place, I came up Drury-lane between twelve and one o'clock, and saw the two prisoners in company; having drank with Baker, before I said, "Will you give me something to drink?" he said, "If you will come with us, you shall have something" - I said, "Where are you going?" he said, "To Field-lane, to sell two handkerchiefs" - they each had a handkerchief on their necks, and when they got to Field-lane, they took them off their necks - Baker went in, and said he had sold the handkerchiefs for 3s.: they then went to sleep in St. Giles' - I slept with a female in the opposite room - I had never seen Hoskins before, but had known Baker about twelve months - I am an unfortunate girl; I saw a silver watch with an engine-turned case, and a guard to it, in Baker's possession, that night; next morning they came into the room where we were sleeping- Hoskins asked me to go and get them some breakfast, which I did, and we all four breakfasted together - Hoskins took the watch from Baker at breakfast time, and returned it again; and between ten and eleven o'clock theywent to West-street, Smithfield - Hoskins gave the watch to Baker in Smithfield; he put it round his neck, and in West-street he took it and put it into his pocket; he went into a house on the left hand side, came out, and said he had sold it for 1l. 4s. 6d. - he gave Hoskins 12s. and the odd 6d. was spent in drink - Baker then said he was going to see somebody in Newgate; we stopped opposite the door; he said he must go and get something to take in to him; he left us; I did not see him again, but on Friday night I saw Baker at the White Hart, and on Sunday evening I saw Hoskins - I saw nothing of him till he was taken up; a Policeman came to me, and I told him what I knew.
JOHN SELLS. I am in the employ of Mr. Turner, pawnbroker, Brydges-street, Covent-garden. I have a pair of shoes, pawned on the 10th of October, between two and three o'clock, in the name of John Moseley, for 2s. 6d.; I do not know who by.
GEORGE MAY. I am a Policeman. I apprehended Hoskins on the 17th of September - he said he was not sorry he was taken; I heard him tell a young man at Queen-square that he had pawned the shoes at Turner's - I heard nothing about the watch; I took Baker at the White Hart, White Horse-yard - I said,"Is your name Baker?" he said, "No, it is Smith;" and denied all knowledge of Hoskins.
ALICE ABRAHAM. I never saw Hoskins with a watch of his own - he was very poor.
HOSKINS - GUILTY. Aged 18.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-235]
2443. HARRIET EVANS was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of October, 1 pair of shoes, value 4s. 6d., the goods of Thomas Perean Morgan.
THOMAS PEREAN MORGAN. I live in Chesterfield-place, New-road. On the 5th of October, about one o'clock, I missed a pair of new shoes from under my window; I went to the first pawnbroker's shop in the same place, and found the prisoner at the counter, with them in her hand - she said they were hers, and she had given a man outside 1s. for them; I asked if she could point him out; she pointed to a man, and when I got up to him she said it was not him, it was another farther on; and when we got to him, she said it was not him - I gave a man in charge, who she said she had them from; the Magistrate discharged him.
CHARLES MUMFORD. I took the prisoner into custody.
Prisoner's Defence. The man asked me to buy them; they would not fit, and he asked me to pawn them.
GUILTY. Aged 27. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18321018-236]
2444. WILLIAM FAWCITT was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of October, 1 picture, framed and glazed, value 5s., the goods of John Pratt.
ELIZABETH PRATT. I am the wife of John Pratt, a fringe-maker, in Exeter-street, Portland-market. On the 17th of October this picture was taken; I had seen it safe in the parlour the day before - the shop door is open in business hours; I never saw the prisoner in the shop - I believe he was in distress.
HENRY BYFIELD. I am shopman to Mr. Gideon, pawnbroker, Stafford-street, Lisson-grove. On the 18th of October I was informed this portrait and frame were stolen, and shortly after the prisoner came and offered it in pledge; I detained him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I bought it.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18321018-237]
2445. WILLIAM JACOBS was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of October, 2 shillings, the monies of John Cross, from his person.
JOHN CROSS. I live in Boswell-court, Queen-square; the prisoner and I lived in the same room, and slept together for three months. On the 18th of October, having suspicion that he was robbing me, I stopped out, and did not go home till six o'clock in the morning; I was employed at some stables - I was quite sober; I put my waistcoat on a chair at the foot of the bed; when he got up, I pretended to be asleep; I saw him take my waistcoat up, and put it down again, and directly he was gone, I missed two shillings; I went out after him - I got a Policeman, and gave him into custody; he denied it; nobody else slept in the room; his clothes were not on the same chair as mine, that I observed.
Prisoner. He came home in liquor; I helped him off with his great coat. Witness. I feigned to be drunk, wishing to find who was the thief - he denied it, but when he got to the station he said he found the two shillings on the carpet; it was day-light; my waistcoat had sleeves, but his had not.
CHARLES WELLDING. I am a Policeman. The prosecutor charged the prisoner with this; I told him to produce his money; he produced two half-crowns and some shillings; among them were two marked shillings, which Cross claimed; the prisoner denied it at first, but said at the station that he found them on the carpet.
JOHN CROSS. These shillings are mine - I marked them overnight.
GUILTY. Aged 32. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18321018-238]
2446. JOHN KNIGHT was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of October, 1 tunic value, 2s.; 1 waistcoat, value 3s.; 1 scarf, value 7s., and 2 belts, value 2s., the goods of William Henry Williams; 3 jackets, value 3s.: 1 waistcoat, value 1s., and 1 shirt, value 1s., the goods of Richard Hughes; and 1 dressing-case, value 2s., and 1 coat, value 18s., the goods of Samuel Johnson .
JEREMIAH MURPHY. I am a labourer, and live in Goswell-street; I am a night watchman at Sadler's-wells. On the 14th of October I went there about ten minutes before my usual time - I put my hand to the back door, which leads into Arlington-street, and it flew open; I found the prisoner inside, with a bundle in his hand; I laid hold of him - he immediately dropped it; it contained the articles stated in the indictment; he was quite a stranger.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am a Policeman. I received the prisoner in charge; I think I had seen him lurking about the theatre at different times.
FRANCIS CAPRANI. I was engaged at Sadler's-wells; some things in this bundle belong to Mr. Williams and others: there are four proprietor s - the things were kept in the dressing-room.
SAMUEL JOHNSON. I am a performer. This dressing-case and coat are mine; I have seen all these other things worn by the different performers.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was in distress, and went to the theatre to sleep all night - I found the things tied in the bundle.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-239]
2447. JOSEPH LEARY was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of September, 1 piece of iron, called a poppet-head, value 13s.; 1 collar and mandle, value 13s.; 2 rest-screws, value 10s.; 2 tops and boxes, value 5s., and 1 iron cramp, value 12s., the goods of William Bradick.
WILLIAM BRADICK. I am a turner and bedsteadmaker; the prisoner was eighteen months in my employ -I was from home when these things were taken.
Prisoner. Q. Did you pay me any wages on Saturday night? A. Every Saturday; I have his signature in my book up to the last fortnight that he worked for me - when I was absent my wife has overpaid him; I never knew him pawn my property.
JAMES ALDOUS. I am a pawnbroker, in Berwick-street. On the 7th of September the prisoner pawned a cramp, for 4s.
JOHN NEEDS. I am shopman to Mr. Chaffers, of Greek-street, Soho. I have a turner's rest and screw, pawned on the 18th of September, in the name of John Williams; I believe by the prisoner.
JOHN REARDON. I live in High-street, Bloomsbury. I have a box and tap, pawned on the 4th of April, but not in my presence.
WILLIAM CLARK. I am shopman to Mr. Jones, High Holborn. I have a collar and mandle, pawned for 4s. - the prisoner does not appear to be the person.
GEORGE HOBBS. I am a Policeman. I apprehended the prisoner on the 5th of October, at work at another shop - I asked where he kept his duplicates; he said he had but one, and that he gave to the prosecutor's wife - it was for the collar and mandle.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. On the day he got my signature to the book, he got a stranger to witness it; after I signed it I brought home work, which he cannot deny; he said,"I have your signature, and you are paid;" I said, "I am not paid for these draw-knots;" he said I should be paid another time - I often had goods given me to pledge, to raise money to pay the men.
WILLIAM BRADICK. I never let him go without the whole of his money; if I was short, I let it go, perhaps, a week - I was in trouble about an action, and the person I left in charge of my business, took his signature; I never asked him for it myself - he did not turn sixteen dozen of draw-knots for me.
JOHN HILL. I am a turner, and live in New Compton-street. The prisoner occasionally worked for me; I lent Bradick a rest, which I never could get back, and this is it - it has been in my uncle's possession fifty years; and, to tell you the truth, all these tools are mine, for I gave Bradick the money to pay for them - I only claim the rest- I consider he will take a false oath.
CHARLES APPLEYARD. I am a porter. I have been in Bradick's employ nine months; he authorized the prisoner to pawn the tools on account of the non-payment of his money, and he owes me money for labour - I have heard him tell the prisoner to pledge his tools, and offer him money afterwards to redeem them; he was taken to the Marshalsea-prison, and owes me 12s.; he sent the prisoner to pawn a top and box-rests, and things- I never heard him authorize the prisoner to pledge, but he knew it was done, and gave him money to redeem them.
- FORD. I am a bedstead-maker. The prosecutor called at my shop between nine and ten months ago, and asked if I had seen Leary, as he had pawned some of his tools, and he wanted him to get them out again.
JOHN HILL re-examined. I know the prosecutor has authorized the prisoner to pawn his own apparel, but I cannot speak about these articles - when he could not get his pay he has made a piece of work, and the prosecutor has offered him his apparel to pledge.
NOT GUILTY.

2449. CHARES JOHN FOX was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of September, 1 pair of spectacles, value 15s., the goods of David Child.
DAVID CHILD. I live in Berkley-court, Clerkenwell. I met the prisoner at a public-house - I had never seen him before; we went home together, and had a game of cards; after he was gone my wife missed her spectacles; the prisoner called the next morning to borrow a pair of spectacles of our lodger, and then my wife asked for hers- he said he had not got them.
WILLIAM HENRY BAYFIELD. I am a pawnbroker. I have this pair of spectacles, pawned by the prisoner on the 25th of September.
MARY CHILD. I am the prosecutor's wife. The prisoner came to our house, and I missed my spectacles when he was gone - these are mine; I had had them ten years.
Prisoner's Defence. I acknowledge pawning the spectacles now produced; but these are my own, and I had had them ever since 1818 - they were made from a fine Brazilian pebble; the pawnbroker knows that I have pawned them before.
WILLIAM HENRY BAYFIELD. I have seen him with a pair - I do not know whether these were them or not.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321018-242]
2450. DAVID GREEN was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of October, 1 book, value 2s. 6d., the goods of John Duncombe.
JOHN DUNCOMBE. I live at St. Giles in the Fields. On the 4th of October I lost a book from my window, which was open; the book stood about two feet in my shop - I do not know whether I was at home at the time; I went up to the Magistrate with another case, and he asked if I hadlost anything else, and he said he had another man in custody, from whom a duplicate had been taken, and it was for my book.
JAMES TRAIL. I am a pawnbroker. I have a book, pawned on the 4th of October; I cannot say by whom - but this is the duplicate of it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I and a friend were coming down Holborn, very much in liquor, he said, "I have a book in pawn, I bought it for a 1s.;" and I said I would bind it in sheep for him.
GUILTY. Aged 45.
2451. DAVID GREEN was again indicted for stealing, on the 6th of October, 3 books, value 1s. 9d., the goods of John Aschon.
BENJAMIN BRITTAN. I am errand-boy to Mr. John Aschon; he lives in Chancery-lane, and is a bookseller. On Saturday night, the 6th of October, I was sitting in the shop, and heard a noise as if some person was taking books; I looked, and saw three books were missing from one corner - I went out and saw the prisoner running; I called Stop thief! and charged him with taking the books; he said he had not got them - he had thrown them down an area; I stopped by him, and gave him in charge.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me take the books? A. No; you were running off.
GEORGE BOULTON (Police-constable F 100). I took the prisoner and found another book on him.
MARY ANN CLARK. I live in Chancery-lane. I was going out, heard a cry of Stop, thief! and saw the prisoner running, and throw the books behind him - I heard the books fall; I took them up - there was nothing else.
Prisoner's Defence. I was walking down Chancery-lane very fast; I passed the shop, and the lad run out and said, "Give me them three books;" I said, "What books?" I kept on walking, and at the corner of Southamptonbuildings he gave me in charge; in going back he said,"These are the books;" I had never seen them.
GUILTY. Aged 45. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-243]
2452. NEIL GOW was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of October, 1 handkerchief, value 2s. 6d., the goods of a man unknown, from his person.
PATRICK HAYES. On the 13th of October I was in Tothill-street, and saw a gentleman walking along; I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief out of his pocket - he then walked down Princes-street; I followed him, and saw him in the custody of the Policeman: the gentleman was gone when we came, and I charged the prisoner with taking it.
Cross-examined by MR. DONNE. Q. Where were you? A. At this end of Tothill-street, on the left hand side, and the prisoner was on the right - I only lost sight of him while he turned the corner to go into Princes-street.
WILLIAM WILLIAMS (Police-constable B 68). In consequence of what Hayes told me I took the prisoner, and found this handkerchief in his right-hand trousers pocket; Hayes said it was the one he took - it is a good silk handkerchief.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going down the street - the Policeman beckoned to me, and said I had picked a gentleman's pocket - I said I had not; he said "Will you go to the station?" I said I would.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.

2458. MARIA KNIGHT was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of October, 1 set of bed-furniture, value 5s.; 2 blankets, value 5s.; 2 pieces of carpet, value 5s.; 1 bolster, value 3s.; 2 pillows, value 2s.; 2 sheets, value 3s.; 1 looking-glass, value 1s.; 1 candlestick, value 1s., and 5 knives and forks, value 2s., the goods of Ann Moorwood.
JOHN PRINCE. I am a private gentleman. I was opposite Mr. Cassell's in Camden-town on the 13th of October, about half-past seven o'clock in the evening; I saw the prisoner carrying a basket, I followed her, and gave information to a Policeman, knowing her before - we then found the owner.
ROBERT BUGDEN (Police-constable S 28). I followed the prisoner into Pratt-street; I took her with this basket; she told me where she lived, and we went there.
ANN MOORWOOD. I am a widow. This basket contains two wine glasses, and two rummers of mine; I also lost two pieces of carpet, two blankets, a bolster, two sheets, and bed furniture - the pawnbroker has them; the prisoner lived in my house nearly three weeks; the whole of this property was let to her - she gave me no warning; I did not miss the property till she was taken.
JOHN WILLIAM CASSELL. I am a pawnbroker; I have a sheet, two blankets, a pillow, and curtains, which were pawned by the prisoner.
WILLIAM JONES. I am a pawnbroker. I was in my shop when the prisoner pawned this bolster, pillow, two pieces of carpet, looking-glass and decanter.
MRS. MOORWOOD. This is my property; she came to me as a married woman, and I let it her with the room; her husband who lived there with her, is a coach harness maker - he came home at night, and went out in the morning; have lost more articles.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that it was her intention to have replaced the property.
GUILTY. Aged 42. - Transported for Seven Years.

2459. BENJAMIN LAMB was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 44 lbs. of flour, value 10s. 6d., the goods of Robert Christopher Payne, his master.
ROBERT CHRISTOPHER PAYNE. I am a baker, at Hammersmith; the prisoner was in my service about three months - I had no other person. On the 14th of September I missed about three pecks of flour out of a sack in my bake house - I have found it since; no one else could have taken it.
JOHN REYNOLDS (Police-constable T 1.) On the morning of the 14th of September I was on duty, about a quarter-past six o'clock; Kelly came, and said he had found a bag, containing a large quantity of flour, in a ground near Mr. Payne's premises - I called Mr. Payne out, and asked if he had lost any flour; he went back, and found he had - I went into his premises, and found a place where a bag had been placed with flour, against a water-butt - I went down the yard, and saw a mark where a bag of flour had been drawn over a wall, nine feet high, and the same on the other side - when I was bringing the prisoner here, he asked me if Counsel would be of any service to him; he said, "I did not place the flour where it was found" - I said, "No, but there is no doubt you drew it over the wall;" he said, "I don't say but I did."
JAMES KELLY (Police-constable T 113.) I found this flour, and gave information to Reynolds.
Prisoner. I am innocent - I had to take flour out of the sack to make my white bread.
R. C. PAYNE. The flour found in the bag corresponds in quality and quantity with what I lost.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Confined Six Months.

2462. WILLIAM McDONALD was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of September, 2 seals, value 7s.; 1 watch-key, value 6d.; 1 chain, value 18d.; 1 purse, value 2s.; 1 tooth-pick, value 2s., and 1 tinder-box, value 1s., the goods of William Cross.
WILLIAM CROSS. I am a waiter, and live in the Haymarket. These things were lost from my bed-room; the prisoner was at work there as a bricklayer's labourer - I missed these and various other articles - suspicion fell on the prisoner, and he was taken.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Had you left the door open? A. The prisoner was at work in the room.
WILLIAM ROGERS. I am a bricklayer. The prisoner was my labourer; the things were lost, and I had suspicion of him; we were at work up stairs at the top of the house.
Cross-examined. Q. How long has he worked for you? A. Only two days.
JOHN HILL. I am a constable; I took the prisoner in Whitecross-street, on the 27th of September; I found this purse, two seals, a key, a German tinder-box, and a toothpick, on him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I am innocent of this - I picked it up outside the door; I exposed it publicly in the taproom, and the men advised me to keep it to see if it was advertised.
GUILTY. Aged 32. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-254]
2463. WILLIAM NEST was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, 2 coats, value 30s., the goods of George Ford.
GEORGE FORD. I am in the employ of Mr Henry Bruce, a window-glass merchant. One of these coats belongs to my fellow- servant, and the other to my master - they were in my master's gig, about a dozen yards from the door of the stable, in Savoy-street - I just turned round and the coats were gone in a minute; I saw the prisoner at a little distance, rolling them up before him - I ran after him, and told him he had got a couple of coats he had just taken off the chaise; he said he had found them down below - they were left in the gig; I gave an alarm, and he was taken.
Cross-examined by MR. DONNE. Q. Neither of them belong to you? A. No - the prisoner was walking up the street; he said he was going to take them to the station - he made some resistance when he was taken.
GEORGE EDWARD GALLIVEY. I was standing in my master's office, at the corner of Savoy-street; I saw the prisoner with the coats across his arm - Ford took them off his arm, and sent for an officer; the prisoner attempted to get off; I said he should not - he struck and kicked me very violently till the officer came up.(Property produced and sworn to).
Prisoner's Defence. I had occasion to go down the Dutch wharf - I saw the two coats lay, and took them up- I was going to the station, and the man came and said they were his master's; I gave them to him, without resistance.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-255]
2464. SARAH PULLMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of October, 1 counterpane, value 2s.; 1 sheet, value 1s. 6d.; 1 tablecloth, value 6d.; 1 pillow-case, value 1d.; 1 hearth-rug, value 1s., and 1 blanket, value 3s., the goods of John Richard Short.
ISABELLA SHORT. I am the wife of John Richard Short. The prisoner lodged ten days in our house; she took the lodging weekly - she went away without notice, and left the key in the door, where we found it the next morning - her husband is a compositor, and did not live with her; she came to me as single; we missed the property stated on the Tuesday morning, and on the Friday she was taken; she owed me one week's rent.
JOHN OWEN ELDRED. I am a pawnbroker. I have a counterpane, pawned for 2s., a sheet 1s. 6d., and a table-cloth and pillow-case for 1s.; I cannot say who by.
MRS. SHORT. This is my property; I lost this and a great deal more, which we have not found; it was all lent to the prisoner.
Prisoner. I know I pawned them, but with the intention of recovering them.
GUILTY. Aged 45. - Transported for Seven Years.

2466. ELIZABETH THOMPSON and ALICE BOREHAM were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 1 gown, value 8s., the goods of Helina Wilson.
HELINA WILSON. I live at Hackney-wick. The prisoners lived in the house next to me; I go out ironing, and I take in washing at home; I do not know what the prisoners are; on the 14th of September, at six o'clock in the morning, I went out, and had left my gown on a clothes-horse in my room, on the ground floor.
REBECCA WILSON. I was at home on the 14th of September; Thompson came in, and Boreham followed her; they asked me to let them boil some potatoes, and while the potatoes were on the fire, they both went out again; Thompson afterwards came and took the potatoes; I did not miss the gown till my mother came home.

JOHN CHEESE. I am a pawnbroker. I have a gown, pawned by Thompson; she was alone.
Thompon's Defence. I did not take the gown.

2467. JOHN WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of October, 1 handkerchief, value 6d., and 1 shilling, the property of George William Barron.
GEORGE WILLIAM BARRON. I belong to the brig Eliza Ann. On the 17th of October she was laying at Woolwich - I was at a lodging house that night, as I had come up with our ships paper's; the next morning, when I awoke, I missed my handkerchief and 1s. out of my pocket - the prisoner had slept there, but he was gone; I had no more money in my pocket, all I had was gone - my handkerchief was found on his neck.
ROBERT EDWARDS (Police-constable K 43). I took the prisoner about eleven o'clock that day; I charged him with robbing the sailor of the 1s., he told me I might go and find it - I found the handkerchief on his neck.
Prisoner. I put the handkerchief on in the dark; I am an entire stranger here.
GUILTY. Aged 40. - Transported for Seven Years.
2468. JOHN WILSON was again indicted for stealing, on the 17th of October, 1 pair of trousers, value 5s., and 1 sixpence, the property of Richard Richardson.
RICHARD RICHARDSON. I am a sailor. I slept in the same room and in the next bed to the prisoner; in the morning, after the prisoner was gone, I missed a pair of trousers and sixpence, which was in the pocket,
ROBERT EDWARDS. I took the prisoner with these trousers on; I believe he had thrown his own old ones away.
GUILTY. Aged 40. - Transported for 7 Years longer.

[trialAccount t18321018-259]
2469. WILLIAM WRIGHT & MARIA WRIGHT were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of October, 1 jacket, value 20s.; 1 waistcoat, value 10s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 14s.; 1 hat, value 12s., and 2 handkerchiefs, value 8s., the goods of John Neal, from his person.
JOHN NEAL. I am a sailor. I came to London by the Henry Brougham, from Palermo - I was discharged at the London-dock; I was paid off on the 15th of October - I was in Essex-street, Whitechapel; I met two men - the male prisoner was not one; I had seen them before, but was not acquainted with them - I got into conversation with them; we went and had some beer - I was at that time sober; we then went to another house, which I thought was a public-house - I there saw the two prisoners; I am not certain whether there was any other woman there but Maria Wright; they sent for a pot of beer - I drank out of it; it was handed round - I do not know whether the others drank or not; I then felt myself all on a sudden very sleepy and drowsy - I could not keep my eyes open; I got up, and said I would go home by myself; I went out, leaving them all in the room - William Wright, and one of the two men who took me into the house, came and asked me to come back, and then Maria Wright came to me, and asked me to stop with her all night - I said no, I would not stop all night; they then would have me go back and finish the beer; I did not consent to go in, but they took me in, and set me down on the bed - I never felt myself so affected before as I was then; I soon fell fast asleep - I cannot tell how long I was asleep, but I think about an hour; I was shoved out of doors by the two prisoners - I had all my clothes on when I went to sleep; when they were shoving me out I had nothing on but my shirt, drawers, stockings, and shoes - I did not hear the prisoners say any thing when they were shoving me out; I sung out for help, went into the room again, and laid down on the bed, and said I would not leave the bed I had been robbed on, till I got my clothes.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What became of the other two men who took you in? A. I do not know, but one of them lent a hand to take me into the house again when I had left it - I had not got above three or four yards from the house when they took me back - I did not go in of my own accord the second time; I did not play at cards - I do not know who went for the beer; it was a small room - I did not notice whether there were any other persons there; two Policemen came to the house.
JOHN WILLIAM DENNIS (Police-constable H 38). I was on duty in Essex-street about a quarter past nine o'clock- I saw the prosecutor there with only his drawers, shoes, stockings, and shirt on; he said he had been robbed in that house - I knocked at the door, and in a few minutes they opened it - both the prisoners were there; the prosecutor said the woman was one who had been in the room - he refused to go to the station till he got his clothes, but he afterwards went, and I took both the prisoners - I found none of the clothes; they denied that the prosecutor was there at all.
Cross-examined. Q. Was there another Policeman with you? A. Yes; but he went away, because the prosecutor would not substantiate the charge - he gave the woman in charge; but I took them both, for he said the man was there, and he had thrown him out of the room - he said two men had taken him into the room; he seemed in a very queer way; I do not know whether he was drunk, or any thing had been given to him - he was very drowsy.
William Wright. I fetched the officer myself. -Witness. No; I was going up the street and saw the people standing and crying shame.
Maria Wright. The prosecutor never came to my door at all, till the officer came, and he knows he found my bed made, and no person had been there. -
Witness. I do not know whether the bed was made or not, for as soon as the prosecutor got in, he went and laid down on it, and said he would not move till he had got his clothes.
W. WRIGHT - GUILTY. Aged 20.
M. WRIGHT - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18321018-260]
2470. MARY ANN WATSON was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of October, 1 tobacco pouch, value 6d.; 1 purse, value 6d.; 1 crown, and 1 half-crown, the property of Peter Evans, from his person; and RICHARD PARKINSON was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing them to have been stolen.
PETER EVANS. I am a Norwegian sailor. On the 21st of October I was in Ratcliff-highway, and saw Watson - she was at a door; I went in with her, and went to bed with her - I had paid her 3s.; she then went away - I then missed my tobacco-pouch, my purse, one crown, and one half-crown from my lefthand waistcoat pocket; I did not see Parkinson.
JOHN MELVILLE (Police-constable K 63). I heard the prosecutor call Police! I went down; Watson was gone: I knew where she lived - I went and found her at her own house; I accused her of robbing the man- she said she had not taken any thing from him, and all the money she had in the world was one halfpenny - I asked her where her old man was, meaning Parkinson; I looked out of the window, and saw him outside - I got him in, searched, and found on him this little hammer, a crown-piece, and a half-crown: while I was doing that Watson ran off - I pursued and took her, and while I did that the serjeant searched Parkinson further, and found on him this purse and this pouch.
PETER EVANS. This is my purse.
Watson's Defence. I was sitting by my fire, he came to the door and asked if he might come in, and stay there; a woman came in - he sent for half a pint of gin, and a pot of porter; he said all he had was 7s. 6d., and a few halfpence - he gave me that; I took it to a woman to take care of for me; she gave it to Parkinson.
Parkinson's Defence. I received it from another woman to take care of.
WATSON - GUILTY. Aged 41.
PARKINSON - GUILTY. Aged 61.
Transported for Fourteen Years.

2473. ANN EDITH KERRIDGE was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of October, 16 yards of ribbon, value 3s., the goods of John Udall.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
ROBERT UDALL. I am superintendent of my father's business at Edmonton - he is a draper - his name is John. On the evening of the 13th of October the prisoner came to his shop, and bought a pair of black worsted stockings; there was on the counter a drawer of black ribbons - she did not offer to buy any of them, but when she was gone I received information, and looked over the ribbons, and missed one, which had been safe ten minutes before - I knew the prisoner; she lived in the neighbourhood; I knew that when she left the shop; she had gone the contrary way to her own house, and she must return past the shop - I sent a witness to stop her; I went and asked her into the shop - I saw her drop this ribbon outside the door, on the pavement; I said she had better tell the truth.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What colour were the stockings? A. Black, and so was the ribbon; there were a good many other ribbons in the drawer, but no other of this width; I put the ribbon back into the drawer - I threw it in - my brother got the constable.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Did the ribbons and stockings stand so near to one another, that she could take one with the other? A. No; the stockings were in paper, and the ribbon in a drawer; there was no other piece of ribbon in the drawer that I could mistake for this; there is about sixteen yards of it.
COURT. Q. Did you sell her the stockings? A. Yes, and she had them in herlap when she came back.
ELIZABETH FRANKLIN. I was in the prosecutor's employ, and was in his shop; the prisoner was at the counter- the stockings were at the end of the counter, in paper, and the ribbons were in a drawer a very little distance from the stockings - I saw the prisoner put her hand into the drawer, take something out, and put it into her lap; I not know what it was.
Cross-examined. Q. How near were the ribbons and stockings? A. A very little distance off - I knew where she lived; there was nothing to prevent her going straight home, but she went a contrary way, and passed our shop again.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Was there any thing that mixed the stockings with the ribbons, so that one could be taken with the other? A. No; the stockings were on her lefthand side, and the ribbons on the right.
JOHN HOCKLEY. I live with the prosecutor. I remember the prisoner coming back; she hurled this ribbon from her apron on the pavement, and I took it up.
Cross-examined. Q. What do you mean by hurling it? A. She threw it on the pavement - my master was near her; it could not be seen by every body in the shop.
The prisoner received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18321018-264]
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
2474. GEORGE MOODY was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of October, 1 dial, value 4l., the goods of Michael Smith Parnther; and 1 umbrella, value 2s., the goods of Edward Breese Robinson .
THOMAS DEVEY. I am constable of Aldgate ward. On Friday, the 12th of October, I observed the street door of Mr. Parnther's house, in Old London-street, ajar; very great violence had been used to break it open; I found no one on the premises, but I found a candle and a match there - there was the appearance of a place where a dial had been; the mortar was quite fresh where it appeared to have been torn from the wall; I found a peg in the back office, on the floor, which appeared to fit a part of the dial-stand.
RICHARD BAYLIS (Police-constable G 64.) At nine o'clock on the night of the 12th of October, I sawthe prisoner go into the door of a shop, in Portpool-lane, carrying this dial under his left arm, and this umbrella in his right hand - he went into Mrs. Brown's - he set the dial and umbrella down; Mr. Cole was in the shop; he came out, and then the prisoner said, "Mistress, I think I have something here that will suit you;" she said, "I don't want to have any thing to do with it;" I went into the shop, and said, "George, what are you going to do with the ticker?" he said, "I know nothing about it" - I called in my serjeant and brother officers; as we were taking him down Portpool-lane - he put his hand into his left-hand pocket, pulled out a phosphorusbox, and dropped it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What was it you called that clock, a ticker? A. I meant this dial - I was close to the shop door, but his back was towards me- I waited to see if the woman bought them - we did not take her.
WILLIAM BARTON. I am a Police-serjeant. Bailey's evidence is correct, but I did not hear the conversation- when I went in the prisoner had his back to the door, and the dial was on the counter.
JOHN HAVERSON. I am clerk to Mr. Michael Smith Parnther. This dial is his property - I have the key; it was on his premises, No. 12, London-street - this umbrella is the property of Edward Breese Robinson, who is his clerk; I left the premises about six o'clock that evening, and shut the door, as usual - no one slept there: the next morning I missed the dial and umbrella - I had seen two or three fellows lurking about, and I believe the prisoner was one.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18321018-265]
2475. EDWARD MARTIN was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of October, 1 waistcoat, value 7s., and 2 handkerchiefs, value 3s., the goods of William Patrick.
WILLIAM PATRICK. I lodge in Lower Gun-alley. On the night of the 6th of October I went to bed, and left my waistcoat and two handkerchiefs in a chair by my bedside- the prisoner slept in the same room; I left him there when I got up next morning - I went up afterwards, and missed the things; the prisoner was then gone.
MARY PETRIE. I am the wife of Robert Petrie; we live in Gun-alley. The prisoner came to lodge with us on the night of the 16th, and borrowed half a crown of me the next morning.
JAMES WILLIAM BARGINALL. I am a waterman, and lodge in Gun-alley. The prisoner engaged me to go and fetch some things for him on the morning of the 17th; he gave me the slip, and I found him just getting into another man's boat - I pursued him, and took him to the Thames Police-office.
JAMES WHITE. I am a Thames Police surveyor. I received the prisoner in charge, and found on him a waistcoat, two handkerchiefs, and 3s.
Prisoner's Defence. I was out of the room an hour before he missed the things - I bought them of a man in the street.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321018-266]
2476. JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of October, 2 jackets, value 1l. 18s.; 2 pairs of trousers, value 1l. 10s., and 4 shirts, value 6s., the goods of Henry Burton.
HENRY BURTON. I am a mariner, and lodge at the Golden Anchor, in Leadenhall-street. I fell in with the prisoner; he had been an old shipmate of mine - he said he was in distress; I told him to call on me, and I would give him some employment - he did so, and I gave him a bag, containing jackets, trousers, shirts, and a variety of other things, to take to the rendezvous house, on Towerhill - I afterwards went there, but he and the things were not there; I received information, and went to the Angel, in Back-lane - I found him there, and part of my things were on his back; all he had on, except his braces, were mine - there are still three shirts, some jackets, and trousers missing.
JOHN EASTBROOK (Police-constable K 25). I took the prisoner; he had these things on - he at first said he had sold the rest to a Jew, in a house; I asked him to tell me where the house was - he then said he sold them to a Jew in the street: I found 6s. 5d. on him, which he said was part of the money he had got for them.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321018-267]
2477. SAMUEL GEORGE was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of October, 1 jacket, value 10s., and 1 pair of trousers, value 10s., the goods of George Thomas Limington: 1 pair of trousers, value 3s., the goods of George Molland; 1 pair of trousers, value 10s., and 1 jacket, value 7s., the goods of Joseph Clare .
GEORGE THOMAS LIMINGTON. I live on board the Hyena. On the 8th of October the prisoner came on board with a jacket, which he asked me to buy; I said it was too small for me - he then showed it to Molland, who also refused it; he then tied it up again - he went on shore with me and Molland; after I had dined I returned to the ship with Molland, I missed my jacket and trousers from my berth - these are them.
GEORGE SEAMAN (Police-constable H 150). I apprehended the prisoner, and asked what he had done with the bundle; he said he should not tell me, for if he had six months in the House of Correction it would only serve to pass away the winter - I afterwards found in a boat belonging to a Dutch vessel, this bundle, which contains the property.
THOMAS WHITSITT. I belong to the Silesia barque, which laid alongside the Hyena. I saw the prosecutor, Molland, and the prisoner go on shore; I afterwards saw the prisoner go on board the Hyena, and then come on shore with a large bundle in his hand; I do not know what were in it.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18321018-268]
2478. ANN WASHINGTON was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of October, 1 sheet, value 18d., the goods of Simon Knee.
ANN KNEE. I am the wife of Simon Knee. He let the prisoner a room in a house adjoining to ours, which we rent - she took it on the Monday evening, and we never saw her again till the Friday.
GEORGE WILLIAMS. I am a pawnbroker. I have a sheet, which was pawned by the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GEORGE SPOTTISWOOD PECK (Police-constable R 143.) I took the prisoner - she said she had pawned the sheet.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 29. - Confined Seven Days.

2479. JAMES HARDY was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of October, 5 planks, value 16s., and 3 deal boards, value 12s., the goods of Edward Rayner.
EDWARD RAYNER. I am the owner of a house at Oldford, in the parish of Stratford; it was under repair, and in the charge of John Vines.
JOHN VINES. I am a carpenter. I had the care of the house - the things were all safe there on the 14th of October, and the next morning I saw through my window some men putting some boards into a cart; I ran to the building, and saw the prisoner in the house, putting the boards and planks out - they belong to Mr. Rayner.
WILLIAM BALDWIN (Police-constable K 201.) I took the prisoner on the spot, taking out the deals.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18321018-270]
2480. CHARLES MITCHELL was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of September, 1 necklace, value 6s., the goods of Charles Collins, from the person of Sophia Collins; and that he had been before convicted of felony.
MARGARET RYAN. I am in the service of Mr. Charles Collins - he lives in High-street, St. Giles'. In the afternoon of the 29th of September I left his house with his little girl Sophia , who is nine months old; she had a coral necklace on, with a gold snap - we went to Stacey-street, St. Giles'; there was a crowd there , but I did not go into the crowd - the prisoner took the necklace off the child's neck ; I gave an alarm , and had him taken.
HENRY CURTIS (Police-constable F 156). I took the prisoner in Stacey-street - I found nothing on him, but he had run some distance , and might have got rid of the necklace.
WILLIAM WETSLEY (Police-constable E 154). I produce a certificate of the prisoner's former conviction, which I got at Mr. Clark's office - I was present , and know he is the person .
The prisoner received a good character from his master, who engaged to employ him.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Confined Ten Days.
[trialAccount t18321018-271]
2481. JAMES PARCELLS was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of October, 3 boots , value 2s. 3d., the goods of James Border.
JAMES BORDER. I am a shoemaker, and live in Church-street, Bethnal-green. On the 16th of October I looked through my window , and saw the prisoner with two other boy s - the prisoner was close to my window , he was pulling a boot through the window , and another lad was cutting the string of it - I followed the prisoner, and kept sight of him , and a man went for the officer ; the prisoner, who is a cripple , was taken, but he had nothing on him - this other boot , which is mine , was brought to my shop by a strange person ; the boot he took down was found about a yard from the spot .
SILVANAS GILL (Police-constable H 55). I took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming past the shop, but did not notice the boots .
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Confined Seven Days.
[trialAccount t18321018-272]
2482. WILLIAM RAYNES was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of October, 7 lbs. of horse-hair, value 5s., the goods of Benjamin Hewett.
BENJAMIN HEWET. I am master of the workhouse of St. Andrew , Holborn - the prisoner was a pauper. On the 21st of October he had leave to go out ; I had some suspicion, and called him back - I found concealed under his clothes 7 lbs. of horse hair ; it exactly corresponds with what we had in the house - when we stopped him, he said , "I am done ."
Prisoner. Our provisions are very slack , and not sufficient for an aged man to do work .
JOHN WILLOUGHBY (Police-constable G 49). I took the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 73. - Confined Seven Days.
